- **[【发布 github】] macos-github-release-no-gh**: 发布 macOS 应用/项目到 GitHub 时 `gh` CLI 未安装。使用 git + GitHub REST API（从环境变量中获取令牌，从未写入磁盘）。内含**完整内嵌的发布 README 模板 V3.1**（居中头部 + 两行八徽章 + 中英镜像，独立可用、不依赖外部模板文件；格式基准为样板仓库 SSHAppInstaller）、**`scripts/check_readme.py` 机器自检**（头部/徽章/章节顺序/占位符残留/隐私泄漏一次判成败）、隐私安全发布（无本地路径/SSH/真实电子邮件/令牌在仓库中）、DMG 资产上传以及使用 python-stdlib 技巧避免 shell `\n` 转义错误。当用户说“发布 github / 推送 github / 发布版本 / 打 tag 上传 DMG / 按某某仓库格式改 README”且 `gh` 不存在时触发。 ([hwl513782273/workbuddy-skills](https://github.com/hwl513782273/workbuddy-skills/tree/HEAD/macos-github-release-no-gh) · ★1)
- **[社交预览生成器] social-preview-generator**: 为 macOS/GitHub 项目批量生成统一风格的社交预览图（social-preview.png，1280×640）。风格源自 AppScheduler 模板：深色垂直渐变背景 + 左侧 App 图标 + 右侧 Light 细字重大标题/主色短下划线/副标题/标语/描边胶囊标签，accent 色自动从图标采样。触发词：社交预览图、social preview、social-preview、README 头图、仓库预览图、批量生成预览图。 ([hwl513782273/workbuddy-skills](https://github.com/hwl513782273/workbuddy-skills/tree/HEAD/social-preview-generator) · ★1)

- **__FRONT_END_PLUGIN__-cancel**: Abandon the current branch's work: close its pull request, delete its remote branch, cancel its issue, and run the repo's teardown. Invoking it authorizes the cleanup. Only for scrapping work without merging, never for landing it. Usage: /__FRONT_END_PLUGIN__-cancel [force|help] ([Project-White-Rabbit/agent-flows](https://github.com/Project-White-Rabbit/agent-flows/tree/HEAD/packages/dev/flows/cursor/front-end/cancel))
- **__FRONT_END_PLUGIN__-configure**: Set up or update .agent-flows/config.json, the repo's shared dev configuration: base branch and the integrations it uses for each capability (source control, issue tracker, deployments, error tracking, …). Usage: /__FRONT_END_PLUGIN__-configure [auto | manual] [local | replace] [section | capability ...] ([Project-White-Rabbit/agent-flows](https://github.com/Project-White-Rabbit/agent-flows/tree/HEAD/packages/dev/flows/cursor/front-end/configure))
- **__FRONT_END_PLUGIN__-create-tickets**: File issues from planning notes, a page link, or a sentence, updating issues that already describe the same work. Not for syncing a branch. Usage: /__FRONT_END_PLUGIN__-create-tickets [match|help] [notes URL | pasted notes | sentence] [--yes] [--tracker &lt;entry&gt;] [--tracker-project &lt;name&gt;] ([Project-White-Rabbit/agent-flows](https://github.com/Project-White-Rabbit/agent-flows/tree/HEAD/packages/dev/flows/cursor/front-end/create-tickets))
- **__FRONT_END_PLUGIN__-evaluate**: Assess branch or PR shippability without edits. Use for readiness and risk assessment, not an audit/fix loop. Usage: /__FRONT_END_PLUGIN__-evaluate [full] [record] [PR number or URL | help] ([Project-White-Rabbit/agent-flows](https://github.com/Project-White-Rabbit/agent-flows/tree/HEAD/packages/dev/flows/cursor/front-end/evaluate))
- **__FRONT_END_PLUGIN__-finish**: Land the current branch's pull request (or its stack): wait for CI on the commit that lands, merge it through the repo's source-control or stacking integration, then close out its issue and errors. Only when the user explicitly asks to land or merge the PR, never for a git merge. Usage: /__FRONT_END_PLUGIN__-finish [stack | branch] [squash | merge | rebase] [no-ready] ([Project-White-Rabbit/agent-flows](https://github.com/Project-White-Rabbit/agent-flows/tree/HEAD/packages/dev/flows/cursor/front-end/finish))
- **__FRONT_END_PLUGIN__-preview**: Open the page the current work changed in a browser, on this worktree's dev server (starting it when it isn't running): the workspace app's browser, else this session's browser tools, else the system's. Usage: /__FRONT_END_PLUGIN__-preview [&lt;page path, URL, or description&gt; | help] ([Project-White-Rabbit/agent-flows](https://github.com/Project-White-Rabbit/agent-flows/tree/HEAD/packages/dev/flows/cursor/front-end/preview))
- **__FRONT_END_PLUGIN__-prototype**: Mock up a UI from a description, as a clickable page in the look of the team's design system, and show it to the person wherever this session can (a hosted page, the team's design tool, or a browser), then iterate on it from their feedback. For exploring a UI, not building production components; it writes in the working tree only when the team keeps its mockups there (pages in `prototype.dir`, a record of each in `prototype.index`), and never commits. Usage: /__FRONT_END_PLUGIN__-prototype [&lt;description of the UI&gt; | design [&lt;design link&gt;] | stop | help] ([Project-White-Rabbit/agent-flows](https://github.com/Project-White-Rabbit/agent-flows/tree/HEAD/packages/dev/flows/cursor/front-end/prototype))
- **__FRONT_END_PLUGIN__-ready**: Take finished branch work to a ready, green pull request: scoped local cleanup and checks, commit, publish, mark it ready, and fix CI with high confidence. Not for merging, reviewing, or one-off checks. Usage: /__FRONT_END_PLUGIN__-ready [loop|draft|help] [--review | --no-review] [--skip &lt;steps&gt;] [--ignore-check &lt;pattern&gt;]... ([Project-White-Rabbit/agent-flows](https://github.com/Project-White-Rabbit/agent-flows/tree/HEAD/packages/dev/flows/cursor/front-end/ready))
- **__FRONT_END_PLUGIN__-server**: Start this worktree's dev server in the background when it isn't running, stop it, list the ones running across the repo's worktrees, or put one started by hand under a watchdog that stops it once its worktree is deleted or nobody uses it. Usage: /__FRONT_END_PLUGIN__-server [up|adopt|restart|stop|down|list|status|help] ([Project-White-Rabbit/agent-flows](https://github.com/Project-White-Rabbit/agent-flows/tree/HEAD/packages/dev/flows/cursor/front-end/server))
- **__FRONT_END_PLUGIN__-start-ticket-queue**: Go through your open issues that have no pull request or workspace yet, most urgent first, and start, skip, or close each one. - **{{skill:batch}}**: Fetch a list of URLs the user already has as one managed Zenrows job, with retries and per-task results. Use when the user supplies or points at roughly five or more URLs (a file, a pasted list, the output of {{skill:map}}) and wants their content or extracted fields. Not for discovering URLs by following links (use {{skill:crawl}}), not for listing a site's URLs (use {{skill:map}}), and not for a single page (use {{skill:scrape-webpage}}). ([ZenRows/zenrows-integrations](https://github.com/ZenRows/zenrows-integrations/tree/HEAD/skills/batch))
- **{{skill:browser-automation}}**: Drive a page through steps before its content is available: clicking, filling forms, logging in, paginating, or multi-step navigation that a single fetch cannot do. Use when interaction is required to reach the content. Not for static page reads (use {{skill:scrape-webpage}}), and not for passively listing the network or API calls a page makes (use {{skill:capture-api-calls}}). ([ZenRows/zenrows-integrations](https://github.com/ZenRows/zenrows-integrations/tree/HEAD/skills/browser-automation))
- **{{skill:capture-api-calls}}**: Discover the background API or XHR endpoints a page calls, or reverse-engineer how it loads its data. Use when the user wants the underlying request a site makes, not the rendered page. Not for interacting with the page UI (use {{skill:browser-automation}}), and not for reading rendered content (use {{skill:scrape-webpage}}). ([ZenRows/zenrows-integrations](https://github.com/ZenRows/zenrows-integrations/tree/HEAD/skills/capture-api-calls))
- **{{skill:crawl}}**: Collect the URLs, and optionally the pages, behind one start URL by following its links, for example "every product in this category" or "all the posts on this blog". Use when the user has one start page and wants many pages from that site. Not for listing a site's URLs from its sitemap (use {{skill:map}}), not for a single page (use {{skill:scrape-webpage}}), and not for a list of URLs the user already has (use {{skill:batch}}). ([ZenRows/zenrows-integrations](https://github.com/ZenRows/zenrows-integrations/tree/HEAD/skills/crawl))
- **{{skill:extract-structured-data}}**: Pull specific data fields from one page rather than its full content: prices, product attributes, article fields, or table rows. Use when the user names the fields they want or asks for structured JSON from a single URL. Not for reading a page''s prose (use {{skill:scrape-webpage}}), not for discovering a site''s URLs (use {{skill:map}}), not for many pages by following links (use {{skill:crawl}}), and not for a list of URLs the user already has (use {{skill:batch}}). ([ZenRows/zenrows-integrations](https://github.com/ZenRows/zenrows-integrations/tree/HEAD/skills/extract-structured-data))
- **{{skill:getting-started}}**: First-run setup and troubleshooting for the Zenrows plugin in {{host_full}}. Use when the plugin was just installed, a Zenrows tool is missing or "not available", Zenrows returns a 401 or AUTH003, the {{connection}} needs sign-in, or the user asks whether Zenrows is set up. Not for normal scraping tasks once the plugin works (use {{skill:scrape-webpage}}). ([ZenRows/zenrows-integrations](https://github.com/ZenRows/zenrows-integrations/tree/HEAD/skills/getting-started))
- **{{skill:map}}**: Discover the URLs that exist on a site without fetching page content, usually from its sitemap. Use when the user wants a list of a site's pages, its structure, or "what's on this site". Not for retrieving page content (use {{skill:scrape-webpage}}, {{skill:batch}}, or {{skill:crawl}}). ([ZenRows/zenrows-integrations](https://github.com/ZenRows/zenrows-integrations/tree/HEAD/skills/map))
- **{{skill:scrape-webpage}}**: Retrieve the content of one specific web page. Use when the user wants to read, fetch, summarize, or get what a single URL says, including JavaScript-heavy pages. Not for discovering the URLs on a site (use {{skill:map}} or {{skill:crawl}}), not for five or more URLs the user already has (use {{skill:batch}}), not for pulling specific fields like prices or emails (use {{skill:extract-structured-data}}), and not for pages that require clicks, logins, or form input (use {{skill:browser-automation}}). ([ZenRows/zenrows-integrations](https://github.com/ZenRows/zenrows-integrations/tree/HEAD/skills/scrape-webpage))
- **{{skill:using-zenrows}}**: Overview of the Zenrows tools for live web data. Use when a task needs the current content of a URL, specific fields from a page, many URLs fetched as one job, the pages linked from a start URL, or a multi-step browser session (login, forms, pagination), especially for JavaScript-heavy pages where a plain fetch returns little. Covers which tool to pick, defaults, errors and plan limits. For a single concrete task, the focused skills ({{skill:scrape-webpage}}, {{skill:extract-structured-data}}, {{skill:batch}}, {{skill:crawl}}, {{skill:map}}, {{skill:browser-automation}}, {{skill:capture-api-c… ([ZenRows/zenrows-integrations](https://github.com/ZenRows/zenrows-integrations/tree/HEAD/skills/using-zenrows))

One spec per invocation; leaves the change staged for the operator to review and commit. - **{{SKILL_PREFIX}}-fix-branch**: Cut one branch for fixing one or several GitHub issues, asking once which branch they belong to by listing the open feature/* branches with main as the last option. A fix for a feature in flight branches from that feature and is tested when the feature merges; a production fix with no owning feature branches from main and must pass pest before it merges, so batching several production fixes costs one pest run instead of one each. Records the target on every issue. Never writes the fix itself. - **{{SKILL_PREFIX}}-merge**: Run the merge for one pull request, a task PR into its feature/* branch or a feature branch into main, with the gates each kind needs: check the gates with definite questions rather than inference, squash-merge on the operator's confirmation, reconcile the local machine, regenerate the documentation indexes from post-merge main, and carry them in a documentation-only follow-up PR.
